\displaystyle d=2e^{i(2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-\delta_{\theta}^{A})}e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}=2e^{i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}}e^{-i\pi/3};

(452a)

\displaystyle u_{3}

\displaystyle\overline{u}_{3}\to Z:

\displaystyle c=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1})(1+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1})e^{-i\pi/3},

\displaystyle d=2e^{i(2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-\delta_{\theta}^{Z})}e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}=-2e^{i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}}e^{-i\pi/3}.

(452b)

For
d
-modes, making use of the explicit expressions of their spinor states, one finds that

\displaystyle d_{1}\overline{d}_{1}:\qquad

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1,5,6\},\ \mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{2,3,4\};

(453a)

\displaystyle{d_{2}\overline{d}_{2}:}\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1,2,6\},\mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{3,4,5\};

(453b)

\displaystyle{d_{3}\overline{d}_{3}:}\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1,3,5\},\mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{2,4,6\}.

(453c)

Then, substituting Eq.(
H.1
) into Eq.(
440
), one finds that

\displaystyle d_{1}

\displaystyle\overline{d}_{1}\to A:

\displaystyle c=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1})(1+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1}),

\displaystyle d=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{A})(e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i\pi})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1});

(454a)

\displaystyle d_{1}

\displaystyle\overline{d}_{1}\to Z:

\displaystyle c=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1})(1+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1}),

\displaystyle d=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{Z})(e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i\pi})=-2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1}).

(454b)

\displaystyle d_{2}

\displaystyle\overline{d}_{2}\to A:

\displaystyle c=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1})(1+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1}+i\delta_{\theta}^{t}),

\displaystyle d=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{A})(e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i\pi}+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})

\displaystyle=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1}+i\delta_{\theta}^{t});

(455a)

\displaystyle d_{2}

\displaystyle\overline{d}_{2}\to Z:

\displaystyle c=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1})(1+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1}+i\delta_{\theta}^{t}),

\displaystyle d=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{Z})(e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i\pi}+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})

\displaystyle=-2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1}+i\delta_{\theta}^{t});

(455b)

\displaystyle d_{3}

\displaystyle\overline{d}_{3}\to A:

\displaystyle c=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1})(1+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{t}),

\displaystyle d=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{A})(e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i\pi}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})

\displaystyle=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{t});

(456a)

\displaystyle d_{3}

\displaystyle\overline{d}_{3}\to Z:

\displaystyle c=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1})(1+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})=2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{t}),

\displaystyle d=\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{Z})(e^{i2\delta_{\theta}^{t}}+e^{i\pi}+e^{i4\delta_{\theta}^{t}+i\pi})

\displaystyle=-2\exp(i2\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}-i\delta_{\theta}^{t});

(456b)

H.2.3
Derivation of Eq.(
181
)

Equation (
181
) is for three-layer modes with
T_{1}
.
For three-layer modes, we have

\displaystyle\vartheta^{t_{1}}_{1}=0,\quad\vartheta^{t_{2}}_{1}=-\delta_{\theta}^{t}/2,\quad\vartheta^{t_{3}}_{1}=\delta_{\theta}^{t}/2,

(457)

\displaystyle\delta_{\theta}^{t}=\pi/3,\quad\delta_{\theta}^{g}=2\delta_{\theta}^{t}.

(458)

For the
t
-modes and
g
-modes, one has

\delta^{t}_{\theta}+\delta^{t}_{\theta}-\delta^{g}_{\theta}=0
and, as a result, Eq.(
438
) becomes that

\displaystyle c=e^{i(\vartheta^{f}_{1}+\vartheta^{f^{\prime}}_{1})}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{z}}1,

(459)

\displaystyle d=e^{i(\vartheta^{f}_{1}+\vartheta^{f^{\prime}}_{1})}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}}1.

(460)

Using these expressions of
c
and
d
, one finds that

\displaystyle t_{1}\overline{t}_{1}\to g_{1\overline{1}}:

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1\}\ \&\ \mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{2,3\},

\displaystyle\to c=1,\ \&\ d=2;

(461a)

\displaystyle{t_{2}\overline{t}_{2}\to g_{2\overline{2}}:}

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1,2\}\ \&\ \mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{3\},

\displaystyle\to c=2e^{-i\delta^{t}_{\theta}}\ \&\ d=e^{-i\delta^{t}_{\theta}};

(461b)

\displaystyle{t_{3}\overline{t}_{3}\to g_{3\overline{3}}:}

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1,3\}\ \&\ \mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{2\},

\displaystyle\to c=2e^{i\delta^{t}_{\theta}}\ \&\ d=e^{i\delta^{t}_{\theta}};

(461c)

\displaystyle{t_{1}\overline{t}_{2}\to g_{1\overline{2}}:}

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1\}\ \&\ \mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{3\},

\displaystyle\to c=e^{-i\delta^{t}_{\theta}/2}\ \&\ d=e^{-i\delta^{t}_{\theta}/2};

(461d)

\displaystyle{t_{1}\overline{t}_{3}\to g_{1\overline{3}}:}

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1\}\ \&\ \mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\{2\},

\displaystyle\to c=e^{i\delta^{t}_{\theta}/2}\ \&\ d=e^{i\delta^{t}_{\theta}/2};

(461e)

\displaystyle{t_{2}\overline{t}_{3}\to g_{2\overline{3}}:}

\displaystyle\ \mathcal{M}_{z}=\{1\},\mathcal{M}_{\overline{z}}=\emptyset,

\displaystyle\to c=1\ \&\ d=0;

(461f)

Finally, substituting the above values of
c
and
d
into Eq.(
131
)
and making use of Eq.(
481
) to be proved later, one gets Eq.(
181
).

H.2.4
Derivation of Eq.(
186
)

From the expression of

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu}=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sigma^{\mu AB^{\prime}}\\
\overline{\sigma}^{\mu}_{A^{\prime}B}&0\end{array}\right),

one sees that

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu{\dagger}}=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sigma^{\mu}_{AB^{\prime}}\\
\overline{\sigma}^{\mu A^{\prime}B}&0\end{array}\right).

Changing indices on the rhs of Eq.(
H.2.4
) by
A\leftrightarrow B
, one gets that

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu{\dagger}}=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sigma^{\mu}_{BA^{\prime}}\\
\overline{\sigma}^{\mu B^{\prime}A}&0\end{array}\right)=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\overline{\sigma}^{\mu}_{A^{\prime}B}\\
\sigma^{\mu AB^{\prime}}&0\end{array}\right),

where Eq.(
270
) has been used in the derivation of the second equality.
Then, one finds that

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma^{f\overline{f}^{\prime}\to B}\gamma^{\mu}\right)^{\dagger}=\left(\gamma^{\mu}\right)^{\dagger}\left(\Gamma^{f\overline{f}^{\prime}\to B}\right)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\overline{\sigma}^{\mu}_{A^{\prime}B}\\
\sigma^{\mu AB^{\prime}}&0\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&d^{*}\\
c^{*}&0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}c^{*}\overline{\sigma}^{\mu}_{A^{\prime}B}&0\\
0&d^{*}\sigma^{\mu AB^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&c^{*}\\
d^{*}&0\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sigma^{\mu AB^{\prime}}\\
\overline{\sigma}^{\mu}_{A^{\prime}B}&0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\Gamma^{f\overline{f}^{\prime}\to B}\right)^{*}\gamma^{\mu}.

(480)

This gives Eq.(
186
).

H.3
A relation between
\Gamma
-matrices of FIPs of antimodes

In this section, we prove the following relation used above,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{f^{\prime}\overline{f}\to\overline{B}}=\Gamma^{f\overline{f}^{\prime}\to B}.

(481)

To be specific in discussion, let us consider the following expressions of the
spinor states of
f
and
\overline{f}^{\prime}
, namely,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{f}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{f}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{i\vartheta_{1}^{f}}|u^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\\
e^{i\vartheta_{2}^{f}}|\overline{v}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\\
\vdots\\
e^{i\vartheta_{n}^{f}}|u^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\end{array}\right)

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{\overline{f}^{\prime}}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{f}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{i\vartheta_{1}^{f^{\prime}}}|\overline{v}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle\\
e^{i\vartheta_{2}^{f^{\prime}}}|u^{\beta}({\bf q})\\
\vdots\\
e^{i\vartheta_{n}^{f^{\prime}}}|u^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle\end{array}\right).

(Other expressions may be treated in exactly the same way.)
The corresponding spinor states of
\overline{f}
and
f^{\prime}
are obtained by replacements of, say,

|u_{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\leftrightarrow|\overline{v}_{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle
, for all the layer states, resulting in

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{\overline{f}}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{f}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{i\vartheta_{1}^{f}}|\overline{v}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\\
e^{i\vartheta_{2}}|u^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\\
\vdots\\
e^{i\vartheta_{n}^{f}}|\overline{v}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{f^{\prime}}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{f}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{i\vartheta_{1}^{f^{\prime}}}|u^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle\\
e^{i\vartheta_{2}^{f^{\prime}}}|\overline{v}^{\beta}({\bf q})\\
\vdots\\
e^{i\vartheta_{n}^{f^{\prime}}}|\overline{v}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle\end{array}\right).

For the sake of clearness in comparing
C^{f^{\prime}\overline{f}\to\overline{B}}_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha\lambda}
and

C^{f\overline{f}^{\prime}\to B}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\lambda}
, let us relabel the spinor states of
f^{\prime}
and
\overline{f}
as

|\mathcal{S}_{f^{\prime}}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle
and
|\mathcal{S}_{\overline{f}}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle
, respectively, i.e.,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{f^{\prime}}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{f}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{i\vartheta_{1}^{f^{\prime}}}|u^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\\
e^{i\vartheta_{2}^{f^{\prime}}}|\overline{v}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\\
\vdots\\
e^{i\vartheta_{n}^{f^{\prime}}}|\overline{v}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\rangle\end{array}\right).

\displaystyle|\mathcal{S}_{\overline{f}}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{f}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}e^{i\vartheta_{1}^{f}}|\overline{v}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle\\
e^{i\vartheta_{2}}|u^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle\\
\vdots\\
e^{i\vartheta_{n}^{f}}|\overline{v}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle\end{array}\right),

Then, it is easy to see that
\overrightarrow{D}|\mathcal{S}_{f}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\mathcal{S}_{\overline{f}^{\prime}}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle

has the same vector-type layers as
\overrightarrow{D}|\mathcal{S}_{f^{\prime}}^{\alpha}({\bf p})\mathcal{S}_{\overline{f}}^{\beta}({\bf q})\rangle
.

Moreover, we note that, for the matrices
T_{k}
used in the studied model of modes,

{T_{k}}
for
{f^{\prime}\overline{f}\to\overline{B}}
is the same as that for
{f\overline{f}^{\prime}\to B}
.
Then, one sees that the values of
c
and
d
for
C^{f^{\prime}\overline{f}\to\overline{B}}_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha\lambda}

are the same as those given on the rhs of Eq.(
124
) for
C^{f\overline{f}^{\prime}\to B}_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\lambda}
.
This proves Eq.(
481
).
